### State-ledger updates required at drafting

Append-only, per `STATE_LEDGER.md` convention — this map does not write the
entries itself, but flags what Movement 1 must establish as opening state
for Book 2: Senna's, Tilda's, and Tonk's confirmed post-diagnosis conditions;
the recovery limits on Rook, Dessa, Wyck, Toren, and Callie; all three
households' residency status; Rook's signed debt; Milo's death as settled grief
entering the book, not news; Callie's and Jab's Ember/reserve states at intake
(both drawn down, per their own cards). Each movement then appends one concrete
medical change for the primary patients and any change in mission fitness for
the recovering party. Do not let a chapter silently reset a body to healthy.

### Continuity risks

- **The Hobb's-Wall draft's "no power inside the wall" rule is not in
  `CANON.md`.** If the owner wants it, it needs to be confirmed as canon
  before Movement 2 depends on it; this map has not assumed it, and instead
  makes the north yard (not "outside the wall entirely") the site where
  supervised training happens, consistent with what Ch. 27–28 already show
  (the north yard as the place "where the work is").
- **Antagonist-figure naming diverges** between the two prior drafts
  (Director Corliss vs. Registrar Helen Quist/Proctor Nye). This map follows
  Director Corliss; if the owner has since preferred the other edition's
  names, that is a one-line fix, not a structural one.
- **Wyck's and Dessa's Book 2 arcs, as drafted in both prior sessions, assumed
  a growth budget this map redirects toward Callie and Jab.** Wyck's and
  Dessa's own material (his Resilience ceiling, her "not just support" arc)
  is worth keeping alive in this book as cohort-level beats, scaled down from
  co-protagonist weight to supporting-cast weight, rather than cut outright.
- **Radiation recall prevents or removes an active burden; it does not erase
  fixed injury.** This protects Senna's established condition and prevents
  Sustain from becoming universal retroactive healing. Exposure windows,
  dosimetry, contamination controls, and ordinary medicine still matter.
- **Usable working depth is not a free power increase.** Training lets a soul
  safely hold and cycle more ash, but intake time, venting, overdraw, physical
  carriage, and the finite ash supply remain hard constraints.
- **The new Riftspawn cannot generate infinite ash.** It recalls ash already
  loosened in the fight or leaking from the rip; once Callie's Ward isolates
  that current and Jab interrupts its Sustain pulse, Toren's damage stays done.
- **Tilda's growth is not repaired by the machine part or by Sustain.** The
  machine supports imaging/treatment infrastructure; her medicines reduce
  swelling and fits. The inoperable growth and her right-side deficits remain.
- **Tonk cannot be mission-portable.** A four-year-old with pins through a
  recently infected fracture does not go to Caul Hill in the old carrying
  frame. His departure scene must preserve his agency without defeating the
  medical facts that made the journey necessary.
- **The 2680 institutions are a lineage seed, not present-day canon pasted
  backward.** Book Two establishes the yard, three methods, and records. Names,
  charters, rivalries, and claims about who founded what remain future-story
  decisions and must reconcile with the final Book Two prose before they are
  promoted from `HORIZON`.

### Reserved truths (deliberately not resolved this book)

- Rook's real name is already delivered (Ch. 27); his third door
  (Resilience) and full unbound status stay open.
- The disk's true origin stays permanently unexplained; this book gets one
  clue, not a resolution.
- Cinder Company's new name/shape: recommend holding it for Book 3's opening
  rather than revealing it on-page here, per both prior drafts' shared
  agreement.
- Whether Director Corliss is a recurring ally, obstacle, or both in Book 3
  stays open.

## 12. Owner decisions still genuinely required

1. **Dee Wren's age and exact door count.** Dee is already canonically the
   Homura survivor Rook walked to safety. Book Two uses Dee as the cross-medical
   teacher rather than inventing a second survivor. Keep age and exact door
   count undisclosed until the owner sets them or the story truly requires them.
2. **Who first identifies Caul Hill.** The destination and merged mission are
   now owner-selected. Recommend Senna name the place herself so the story
   does not use her history without her agency, but the scene staging remains
   open.
3. **The new Meridian-native cohort member's identity** — recommended above
   to keep the cohort from reading as "the Book One cast plus scenery,"
   entirely open for invention at drafting.
4. **Which household carries Movement 5–6's named residency injustice**, and
   whether it is the displaced family found at Caul Hill or a separate
   Meridian household — both work; the owner may prefer to reserve this for
   the drafting author's discretion rather than lock it here.
5. **Whether any romantic thread is wanted** across Callie, Jab, and Toren.
   Book 1's tonal register keeps romance secondary if present at all
   (`CANON.md`, Audience and tone); this map has not assumed one exists.
6. **Confirm or reject the working title "Meridian" against "The Ledger".**
   The map retains *Meridian* because that is the owner's stated Book Two
   title; only a later direct ruling should change it.

## 13. Recommended final book length

**Owner target: approximately 300,000 words.** Movement One established the
actual scale at 39,707 words across eight chapters. Keep six major movements,
use eight-chapter creative runs for Movements Two through Six, and allow the
remaining movements approximately 48,000–55,000 words each when their scenes
earn that room. The resulting forty-eight-chapter book should land roughly in
the 285,000–315,000-word range. This is an allocation, not a padding quota:
spend the added room on developed teaching, medical progression, relationships,
travel consequence, and trackable action rather than repeated explanation.

---

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — books/book-02-meridian/packets/MOVEMENT-003.md

# Movement brief — Working Depth

- PWA book id: `kindled-book-02-meridian`
- Series id: `kindled`
- Author: Opus 5.5
- Seat: Monroe Jackson 1.3.0 / O'Connor
- Chapters: 17–24
- Intended movement scale: approximately **46,000–52,000 words**, so the
  cumulative book finishes near 150,000 words at midpoint and remains on course
  for roughly 300,000 words. Preserve developed scenes and human consequence;
  do not pad to reach a quota or compress the final chapters to fit one response.
- POV: Jab-leaning, with Callie and Toren co-equal behind him. All three remain
  protagonists and causally necessary. Use single-viewpoint chapters, no head-hop.

## Where we enter — the next moments

Movement Two ends on D+28 with Callie, Jab and Toren on the hut steps, the machine's
second yellow light waking, Caul Hill named, and the arithmetic of a deep-zone
mission finally visible. Begin in the next lived moments—same night or the next
morning. Do not hide the Sag failure's cost behind a time jump or recap it as a
report.

Read chapters 1–16 together before writing forward. Treat
`provenance/MOVEMENT-002-CONTINUITY.md` as the current state ledger. Carry every
body, clock, object, debt, promise, and knowledge boundary. In particular:

- Jab cannot find his bucket after going through his floor. His hands shake; the
  Director's medical-register offer is standing; his public value is now visible.
- Callie kept only a finger above her floor, has two lost wage days, healing ribs,
  and a Ward that can cover one other person for eleven seconds at high cost.
- Toren's left forearm remains splinted, his right palm has fresh stitches, and his
  one-breath Guard is not yet repeatable under load. He has admitted that he saw the
  holder leave and failed to call it.
- Rook is flat after holding the Sag for thirty-one minutes. Dee's quarter-Handful
  stilled his hands briefly; neither fact restores him.
- Tonk's infection is clear but his frame remains until spring and the growth-plate
  damage remains. The cord runs from his wrist to the empty sixth-bed rail.
- Tilda's medicine buys uneven mornings but does not cure the growth, right-side
  failures, language gaps, or fits. She retains authority over her own prognosis.
- Senna's next cycle is D+32. Her hands and feet are numb; she must be told where
  the floor is. She named Caul Hill and can plan the road she cannot walk.
- The day holder will not hold this winter. Rook is the only remaining holder;
  the Sag is worsening. The machine is on cycles only and has two yellow reference
  boxes. Caul Hill is six days north and west on the old line.

The historical conflicts over Wyck's injured side/finger count, the single-account
wording for Toren's original arm break, and Tonk's six-versus-ten-week estimate are
owner-level issues. Do not silently resolve or amplify them in this movement.

## What this movement is for

Turn the idea of a Caul Hill mission into a measured operation that can plausibly
leave Meridian. The Director initially refuses it because Meridian cannot spare its
only holder and because the proposed party cannot yet state an ash budget, exposure
window, healer rotation, abort line, or home-defense plan. This refusal is practical,
not villainous. Rook and Dee prepare the evidence anyway: one teaches the bodily
feel of Sustain, the other teaches reproducible capacity and stopping.

The movement's pleasure engine is controlled improvement. Use attempts, bodies,
objects, disagreements, and consequences to dramatize ash ladder stages 5–9:

1. **Taking ash in.** Skin-contact absorption is deliberate and unhurried. The soul
   holds it as usable reserve; there is no second organ or game inventory. Rushing
   creates spill, nausea, false fullness, or a lock that prevents clean spending.
2. **Working depth.** Intake, settle, spend, vent, rest, repeat. A soul does not grow
   metaphysically larger in a week. Usable depth means clean repeatable cycles and
   recovery, not forcing in the largest possible quantity once.
3. **Radiation recall.** Sustain senses and draws only the active harmful burden in
   a living body while ash pays for separation. It cannot reverse fixed damage,
   regrow marrow, cure Tilda, straighten Tonk's leg, or undo Senna's old injury.
   Physical decontamination and ordinary medicine still matter.
4. **Venting and rotation.** A practitioner must vent the recalled burden before it
   lodges in them. One works, one holds reserve/support, and one watches the clock,
   symptoms, spill, and abort line. Continuing past safe depth makes the healer the
   next patient even if ash remains in the room.
5. **Spending through a door.** Settled ash can fund a known door without first
   emptying personal reserve. Callie, Jab and Toren should each discover one useful
   economy or constraint through practice, not receive a generic power increase.

Jab is the movement's progression center. He has to accept instruction from the
same institution that is trying to put a labor claim around his gift. Let him set
terms: Tonk and his household are his boundary; learning how not to vanish into a
patient is not consent to belong to the register. The Director may record the
value of his ability, but she cannot own it.

Rook must admit that Homura had a repeatable capacity method he never fully learned.
Dee must admit that protocol without Rook's relational feel cannot teach Jab what
to find in a body. Their disagreement should produce a better method, not a winner.

## The other two progression lines

Keep all three protagonists learning together often enough that their Ember remains
visibly personal:

- **Toren:** Jab coaches edge-light economy—lighting only the useful section of the
  crooked spike and holding it through impact. Toren continues building Guard from
  Stride: from the ground up, soft behind, structural rather than Ward. This movement
  may achieve a repeatable braced hold under controlled load, but the full moving
  field payoff belongs later. Do not straighten his shape or erase the late left
  strut that taught him the correction.
- **Callie:** refine the long hooked Edge she already owns and practise a deliberate
  Ward-to-Edge handoff. She cannot run full Ward and full Edge everywhere at once.
  The work is knowing which load to hold, when to let it transfer, and where to cut.
  Do not give her a new straight blade or group-scale mastery early.
- Use at least one practical combined drill in which Jab's economy, Callie's handoff,
  and Toren's brace solve different parts of one changing load. Let a mistake alter
  the next attempt rather than handing everyone immediate success.

## Controlled exposure becoming a real emergency

The movement's substantial action/emergency sequence is not a fight and must not be
a contrived child test. Meridian already possesses failing calibration hardware and
an unnamed grey-haired technician established in Movement Two. A plausible route is
a low-source calibration piece in an old shielded housing, brought under Dee,
Sowerby and the technician's control so Jab can learn what an active burden feels
like at the edge of safety. The author owns the exact hardware and staging, but it
must fit the post-Fall world and ordinary contamination practice.

The control fails for a physical, planted reason—old seal, cracked housing, dropped
carrier, contaminated worker, or comparable failure. It exposes an adult/technician,
not a disposable unnamed child. Establish positions, shielding, handling rules,
available ash, who watches time, who can touch whom, and the abort line before the
failure. Then let the emergency change the plan.

Jab and Rook perform the first paired radiation recall. Dee controls the rotation
and vent; Sowerby/ordinary medicine handles contamination and injury. Callie and
Toren solve necessary physical containment/access problems within their present
limits, rather than standing aside. The result proves recall works and also proves
an improvised trip will run out of clean ash. Nobody is miraculously cured. The
exposed person survives with a specific, carried consequence.

This sequence should be developed enough for comprehension, fear, adaptation,
cost, and recovery. It is not the Caul Hill climax and should not imitate a monster
fight.

## Medical clocks and departure heartstrings

Patients continue to live through the preparation; they are not props waiting at
the start and finish.

- **Tonk:** give him one small, medically credible independent movement after the
  trapeze-bar gain—transfer partway to a chair, pull himself upright, or another
  effort that leaves the frame and damaged leg true. He remains four: funny, angry,
  frightened, and literal. At departure he assumes the old carrying frame means he
  can go. The answer is no. He releases Jab's end of the cord by his own choice and
  entrusts the wooden fish or another already-established object as a promise to
  return. He must not be carried to Caul Hill.
- **Tilda:** show at least one clear morning and then a bad one close enough to
  departure that Callie means to stay. Tilda, lucid and acting with authority,
  refuses to be made the reason Callie abandons the mission that keeps the shed
  functioning. Return her `stop holding` lesson without turning it into a speech or
  surrendering her right not to hear the prognosis number.
- **Senna:** D+32's cycle happens. Show a concrete loss of hand/foot function and a
  concrete gain in institutional authority. She builds or dictates the first route
  and exposure ledger for Caul Hill, supplies a detail or existing object only she
  could provide, and names that this is a road she can plan but never walk.
- **Rook:** ability returns by functions—rise, short hold, one rotation, paired
  recall—not by reset. His reduced margin must appear in the mission budget.
- **Dessa and Wyck:** staying at Meridian is a necessary home command, not being
  discarded. Dessa's record/observer work and Wyck's body-discipline lane are the
  beginning of a durable yard. Keep their injuries real.

Intercut or otherwise connect the two hardest releases: Tonk letting Jab leave and
Tilda sending Callie. Toren witnesses enough to understand that leading means
returning people to the beds and rooms that made them go. Do not kill a patient to
manufacture permission.

## What the operation must know before departure

By Movement Three's end, the manuscript—not just a planning file—must make the
following legible through argument, trial, ledger, packing, or decision:

- a finite graded-ash budget and how much is committed before any Caul Hill ash can
  be won;
- healer roles and rotation order for Rook, Dee and Jab;
- exposure window, vent procedure, symptoms/measurements, and the abort line;
- Callie and Toren's physical protection roles;
- coaches/adults travelling and people holding Meridian's home line;
- why the calibration part and ash-rich rip are one mission, not two errands;
- what happens to the mission if Rook cannot safely complete his assigned share;
- the condition under which the Director changes `no` into a narrow, accountable
  permission—or the exact ethical reason the party leaves without formal approval.

Prefer earned conditional authorization after the team produces a credible plan;
do not make the Director foolish simply to create rebellion. Whatever the author
chooses becomes canon and must be carried into the continuity checkpoint.

## What must remain true / withheld

- Public byline is Monroe Jackson. O'Connor remains internal.
- Callie, Jab and Toren are co-protagonists despite the seat's normal single-lead
  ratio. This movement leans toward Jab without reducing the others to helpers.
- Do not introduce a new continuing named character. Existing unnamed station roles
  may appear and remain unnamed. The owner names continuing cast.
- Dee's exact door count stays undisclosed. Rook's third door stays undisclosed.
- No romance is required or assumed.
- No Cinder reveal, disk explanation, Sustain-echo creature, full group Ward, full
  moving Guard, or Caul Hill creature fight yet.
- The mission may depart at the movement's final seam; the road itself belongs to
  Movement Four.
- Ash is finite. Working depth is not rank. Radiation recall is not retroactive
  healing. A healer can be harmed by what they recall.
- Preserve Milo's grief in lived objects or habits without declaring it resolved.
- Keep the future schools as a seed: lanes, records, disagreements, and methods,
  not a chartered academy or future names pasted backward.
- No copied wording, names, proprietary terms, or scene sequence from the Iron
  Prince research. Use only the selected abstract numerical craft formula.
- Prose must read cleanly aloud: clear referents, punctuation that exposes causal
  hierarchy, thought-level paragraphs, and controlled long sentences. Avoid a
  return to repeated descriptive epithets once names are known.

## What the author owns

Own all eight chapter boundaries and titles, the exact single-POV sequence, the
Director's refusal and reconsideration, the controlled-exposure apparatus and
failure, the practical drills, humor, arguments, recovery, packing, and departure
seam. Plans describe the required change; discover how these people create it.

Write chapters 17–24 as one continuous creative run. Do not stop for per-chapter
editing, scoring, or approval. If the output limit intervenes, save complete
chapters and a precise continuation checkpoint, then resume the same Opus session
and movement. Do not shorten later chapters merely because earlier chapters ran
long.

## Where we leave pressure

Leave Meridian with a measured expedition crossing the gate or poised to cross it,
carrying finite ash and specific promises. Radiation recall has worked once under
controlled conditions and nearly cost too much. Jab has terms rather than ownership,
Callie has chosen trust over holding everything herself, and Toren has begun to
understand leadership as a return obligation. The people staying behind are active
participants with home-line work. The road, the rip, and Caul Hill remain ahead.

The final feeling should be departure bought by preparation and love—not a dry
mission briefing and not children running away because adults would not listen.

---

## The Sag-line failure (D+25), as staged

- **Positions.**
  - South lip: Pell (whistle, a lent watch), Dessa (count), Wyck (not cleared), and the day holder in her hollow holding the sheet.
  - North lip: Rook, due to take the line at minute 20.
  - East track: Katori guarding; Callie and the weighbridge girl lowest, just above post 5; Otto and two yard lifters above them. Nobody lifts below post 5.
  - Toren runs jars. Jab stands in the west door. Ines is on the west rim above the pipe's west mouth with her bow.
  - Dee is at the yard gate, 200 paces back, by the Director's rule (objected to by D. W. and P.), with the kit, the stretcher crew, three graded jars of Flask, and a Handful already settled in her. Her "two minutes, three in fog" is for the whole party (bag, jars, two stretcher-bearers) and includes stopping for the first injured person she reaches, not a runner travelling light.
- **Success, as written:** the lift up in 40 minutes; nothing through the line; nobody below their floor; everybody out on three blasts.
- **The sequence:**
  1. Minute 18: warm iron at post 5 and two shadows (Toren); "It's going" (Callie). Katori moves the lifters ten paces up. Toren shouts "Six!"; Pell blows one "listen" blast.
  2. Minute 20: Pell calls the changeover. The holder refuses it with six moving (letting go to hand over would open a gap on six) and shouts "Keep your hands down, Marsh." Rook keeps his hands down. Nobody sets a new minute. The ledger records this as a failed decision.
  3. Minute 21: the slab at 6 drops, the sheet folds at the east corner ("Weight!"), and husks surge up. Toren, looking up the track, sees the holder leave her hollow and go down alone into the fog toward the corner. He does not call it.
  4. Three blasts. Callie, Toren and the girl are cut off below the surge and cross the bottom west. The girl falls correctly (wrist). Toren drops the jar bag, and a Flask and more is lost.
  5. Jab lights his knife to draw the husks, lighting through the belly with no gutter.
  6. The hounds go for the dark, into the pipe. Callie reads the stopped water and wards the hook across the west mouth; she has nothing on her. Toren's light fences the arch.
  7. Jab feels the lean at forty yards, hands the door to Toren ("She's worse") and crosses dark.
  8. The day holder was hit at the corner after the fold. Nobody saw the hit. She is holding one-handed ("Get back to your post, boy"). She collapses and the sheet goes. Rook takes the whole bottom alone, for 31 minutes.
  9. Jab holds her nine minutes and goes below his floor.
  10. The hounds come back east, to the dark. Toren arrives with Stride and fences them; Katori kills both. Asked "Where's the medic", Toren reports only what he can know: three blasts went, so she will have come to the lip.
  11. Dee arrives. She came up the road with kit and stretcher crew, stopped at the lip to do the girl's wrist ("nobody had the holder on the count"), and came down when Wyck asked "where's the holder" (her account, ch. 16). She takes over on settled ash and lifts Jab's hand off. Jab collapses.
- **Callie's part:** the hook held to her floor at 58 s. The hounds had gone quiet by about 30. Katori came down the rim and said "out".

## Object state

- **The copied plate:** Toren's coat pocket, on the other side from the stone.
- **Dessa's ledger:** Pell's spare, with a cracked spine.
  - The first page holds "Say it out loud. Even when it's awful. — M. F. On the wall."
  - It also holds the map, the D+25 page with its columns, and Senna's why ("Because the ones who hold the line are the ones nobody watches").
  - The D+25 page's capital line is "THE HOLDER WAS HIT AT THE FOLD, IN THE FOG. NOBODY COUNTED HER." Under it: "T. saw her leave the hollow at the fold. Did not call it." The timeline records the minute-20 changeover not made and no new minute set.
  - Under *what to change* it adds a changeover rule and "The runner calls anybody who leaves their place." Rook's closing line is "COUNT THE HOLDER".
- **Dee's book** is separate. It holds "Floor: a finger. Did not say yes."
- **Dee's brass one-minute sand-glass:** "the only thing I took off the wire that wasn't on my back".
- **The long cord:**
  - Since D+25 it has run from Tonk's wrist to the rail of the empty sixth bed on the right, in a knot a nurse tied twice.
  - Tonk has said nobody is to untie it.
  - Jab went home to twelve on D+28 without it.
- **The fish ("Little"):** it was on Jab's ward pillow on D+25–26; it is not tracked since.
- **Tonk's frame:** it now has a trapeze bar on a chain.
- **The weighbridge girl's tally board:** now on Tilda.

## Open clocks at exit (D+28)

- **Machine:**
  - The fourth box is drifting and a fifth box, next along, is now waking slow.
  - On D+21 the tech said "four weeks, five" before he stops or guesses; on D+28 he said "less".
  - The machine is on cycles only; pictures are taken only if they would change treatment.
  - Senna's next cycles: D+32, D+43, then about D+54.
- **Reviews:** Callie's and Tilda's household on D+41; Jab's household on D+42.
- **Rook:** his four-season debt continues; half his teaching hours count against it.
- **Wyck:** re-strapped on D+14; Keel says six weeks, about D+56.
- **Jab:** the Director's medical-register offer is standing ("I'll ask again"); Jab declined while unwell.
- **The Sag:** arrivals of 11, 14, 19 and 23 a week. The day holder is out for the winter at least, so Rook is the only holder.
- **Caul Hill:**
  - Named by Senna: six days north then west on the old line (red in Pell's atlas); a hill with its top taken off; eleven miles of fence; instrument rooms of racks bearing the same mark; hundreds of boxes thrown on the floor in 2130, "heavy and warm".
  - The Director reported the sky "wrong" over "the old plant" since the summer, with unlifted ash lying in drifts.
  - Not launched. No mission is proposed on the page.

## Bodies at exit

- **Tonk:**
  - The infection is clear (D+16). The growth plate is worse than first said, so a higher boot and a mid-growth operation are more likely.
  - The frame stays until spring.
  - He first sat up by himself on the bar on D+25, while Jab was unconscious.
- **Tilda:**
  - The pills help her mornings. She found "thistle" and loses words for about 3–4 s.
  - Her right hand drops things, and her right leg gave way on D+13 and D+20.
  - She has had no fits since the 41-second one.
  - She works the weighbridge tally left-handed by her own choice and spoke for her own household.
  - She gets no four-week picture.
- **Senna:**
  - Both hands and both feet are numb; she has to be told where the floor is.
  - Course and cycles continue.
  - She is the yard's reasons-keeper, dictating to Toren, and named Caul Hill by her own choice.
- **The day holder:** her spleen was removed (Sowerby had her open by the ninth hour on D+25). She will live. She won't hold this winter, maybe not again. Her coat was returned on the Director's order at Jab's request.
- **Rook:** held the whole bottom alone for 31 minutes on D+25 and is flat. His hands were stilled for a while by Dee's quarter-Handful on D+26. Sowerby is furious with him.
- **Toren:**
  - The left forearm is still splinted and no worse.
  - The right palm seam reopened on D+25 and was stitched.
  - Stone warm; floor measured once at 2¼ turns dark.
  - Guard: a correction he can retry ("from the ground up; soft behind"), holding for one breath unloaded.
  - His spike light has held through one fall.
  - He saw the holder leave her hollow and did not call it. He owned this aloud in the shed on D+26, and it is in the ledger.
- **Callie:**
  - Healing ribs, re-aggravated.
  - Nosebleeds on D+20 and D+25/26.
  - Her floor is kept, a finger above it.
  - Two lost wage days on the sheet (D+20, D+25).
  - Her handoff gap is two; her Ward walks two steps; her one-person widening lasts 11 s.
- **Jab:**
  - Went through his floor on D+25 and cannot find the bucket at all as of D+28.
  - His hands shake, and he went home against advice.
  - Publicly known ("SUSSTAIN" on the board; a mother came asking for him).
- **Wyck:** collarbone re-strapped D+14; not cleared; teaching the body lane.
- **Dessa:** her leg is bad on slopes. She learned that thirty feet is not the bottom of a dip.
- **The weighbridge girl:** broken wrist, set by Keel; fell correctly.
- **Otto:** the forearm that was stitched is itching as it heals.

## Deliberately absent or withheld

- **Paid off later, not here:** Guard (only an accident, a failed attempt and a one-breath standing test); group-scale Ward (one person for 11 s only); radiation recall (not taught; implied only through Senna's history and Jab's arithmetic); the Sustain-echo creature; the disk (not mentioned); Cinder material (only Dee's oblique line that the Homura survivors had heard what Rook was doing); the Caul Hill expedition.
- **Katori's story:** Katori's "why the same word" is untouched. Dee recognising Vera in Katori's face is planted in a single silent beat (Dee looks, Rook nods, Callie infers and says nothing).
- **Nothing named:** Dee's door count, Rook's third door, and all Director, trainee, day-holder, technician and long-coat names.
- **No romance.**
- **Milo's grief** is carried by the bread, Senna's nightly naming, Tonk joining it, and the ledger's first line. No grief speech is declared resolved.

# Movement Three — continuity checkpoint

Author run: Claude Opus 5.5 (`claude-opus-5-5`), Monroe Jackson 1.3.0 / O'Connor 1.3.0.
This is a working note for movement review and Movement Four planning. It is not
manuscript text, and it is not an editorial score. It supersedes
`MOVEMENT-003-WORKING-PLAN.md`, the pre-drafting plan, wherever the two differ.

## Author choices that are now canon

| Question | Resolution on the page | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Handfuls in a Flask | A clean Flask is eight Handfuls at the weighbridge; a thin one is nearer seven. It is written inside the store book's cover | 19 |
| Where the mission's ash comes from | The station store under the weighbridge, lent rather than given. It held 31 Flasks and 2½ Handfuls (4 thin, 27 clean) on D+34. Twelve clean Flasks were weighed out by Tilda on D+39; she sent two thin jars back. The board reads *LENT. CAUL HILL. 12 FLASKS, CLEAN. J., 8, IN THE FRAME. P., 4, ON THE CART. ROAD 1. HOME 3.* | 19, 21, 23 |
| Rook's prior recall | On Senna, by feel, for three hundred miles on the road, fuelled by a flask of road-ash. He vented into the ground through his heels without knowing he was venting. It made his hands ache for an hour afterward. It is why Sowerby's "it shouldn't have worked" held | 19, 20 |
| Why Rook lacks the capacity method | At Homura, Vell's long table belonged to the medical line (nine children the year Rook came). Rook was in the doors line with Vera and Ander. At fifteen he called the table tedious behind Vell's chair; Vell hit him across the legs with his stick and said he'd have to learn it off a child | 17 |
| How Dee learned what to find | Every spring Vell held his own forearm to a small lead-housed source for a count of sixty and had the new medical children find and draw it. Dee sat at the table "for the months I had there" (this fits "since I was fourteen") | 19 |
| The exposure apparatus | The dead reference box from row one, nine along (the one Toren carried on D+11). Its reader was dead and its sealed source was live, weakened by seventy years. The planted failure: the technician had taken seven of its eleven back-plate screws over twenty years "for the rack" | 19, 20 |
| What active burden feels like to Jab | "The busy thing that isn't them": warm, not the person's own warmth, like a stone left in the sun or a fine warm grit. Fixed damage feels like Dessa's "old leaning wall", which is not busy and cannot be drawn | 20 |
| Recall mechanics as staged | Draw like lifting a drift: flat palm, no hauling. Ash paid in through the left hand passes out through the right and loosens the burden, which comes up into the right palm as prickling heat. Hold it high so it does not go down into you, then vent it into spent, colourless ash in a vent jar. Clean in the left, dirty out the right, never the same shelf. The watch draws the leftovers out of the worker before the worker rests | 20 |
| Signs of lodging | The healer's palms go red "like he'd held a pan", and the counter reads high at the palm after venting. Rook lodged at about the 20th minute and came off at the 27th | 20 |
| Jab's door | It passes ash even with an empty reserve (D+29). "Carrying" means holding ash high in the palm, and he managed 11 breaths on D+29, 20 on D+33 and 40 by D+39. His pass-through jar method, left hand on the jar and right hand on the patient, never touches his own reserve until the jar runs dry, and then it takes him instantly | 17, 19, 20 |
| Callie's lock | She settles very well, then cannot spend what she has settled. It released during the combined drill (D+34), when she let the Ward go while Toren held the load. From then on she can fund the hook's Ward from settled ash: 1½ turns on a quarter-Handful (D+34), 1¾ turns (D+39), and an estimated ~7 minutes on a settled Handful. On herself the Ward costs about three times as much, and she cannot have it on herself and in the hook at once | 19, 23 |
| Toren's intake | The slowest in the yard: a tenth of a Handful in 104 breaths, and a Handful in "most of an hour" the night before departure. He must always be "the first one full, the night before". A tenth of a Handful settled funds about four soft holds at six paces | 18, 24 |
| Settling times, others | Callie takes about 30 breaths for a tenth, ~70–75 for a quarter and ~300 for a Handful. The weighbridge girl takes about 40 for a tenth and ~100 for a quarter | 18, 19, 23 |
| Ward and radiation | **Not stated on the page.** On the hill Callie's Ward holds doors and stairs against creatures only. Recommend the owner keep it that way: Ward does not shield radiation | — |
| The dummy | Otto named the straw training dummy "Norris". It is a sack, not a person. Flagged in case the owner counts it as a name | 19 |

## The north-gate pen (D+35), as staged

- **Positions:**
  - The box on an iron depot stool 3 ft from the drain, window toward the gate end, inside a 6-ft chalk ring.
  - The tech inside the ring, with elbow gloves and tongs.
  - Dee kneeling outside the chalk with her right forearm bare; Jab beside her; Rook on a crate behind Jab.
  - Six clean Flasks on the bench; three vent jars of spent ash on the floor by the gate wall.
  - Callie on the bench at the hatch end, 30 ft off, with the counter and Dee's glass.
  - Sowerby at the hatch end with a basin, cloths, scissors and blankets. Hallet behind his window.
  - Toren at the pen gate as runner; two grey coats outside with the hose.
- **Rules said aloud and written:** only the tech's hands on the box; only Dee's arm at the window; one turn of the glass; ticks every ten breaths; *red* means everybody out; *shut* means the arm comes away; Jab touches only Dee and Rook touches only Jab; clean in the left, dirty out the right; *vent*, *off* and *floor*.
- **The planned exposure:** 40–44 ticks per ten breaths at the bench over one turn. Jab felt the burden in Dee's arm without touching it.
- **The failure:**
  1. The slide stuck a third closed. The tech shoved, the box turned, and the four-screw back plate sheared.
  2. The lead plug and the capsule fell out. The box tipped, and the tech caught it on his right hand.
  3. The capsule split on the drain grating and grey-white powder spilled, some of it into the air.
  4. The counter went to a buzz; Callie called *red*.
- **Containment:**
  1. Dee: "Nobody breathe it", and Sowerby threw wet cloths.
  2. Toren Strided in and put up a ground strut against the rocking box: "Holding".
  3. Callie came down the bench side, reading the floor ("It's a bowl… Bench side's clean"). She used the hook as a lever over the stool rim to lift the box's front edge 1½ in, then put the Ward in the hook: "Taking" / "Yours" / "Mine".
  4. Jab pulled the tech's hand out; the glove stayed behind. He dragged the tech by the collar along the bench side to the hatch end.
  5. Toren went back under the box: "I'm under it". Callie released the Ward into a short Edge on a count of two. From five feet she cut the powdered sleeve open cuff to shoulder without touching skin, and Sowerby stripped the coat.
  6. Toren walked the lead case on its edge over the capsule. The counter fell from a buzz to 90 per ten breaths.
- **Recall:**
  - Sowerby washed the tech first. Dee: "Rook works, Jab under him, I'm the watch and the reserve, Callie the glass and ticks, Toren the jars." Start 9:23 in the evening by Hallet.
  - Chest ticks: 140 → 120 → 100 → 85 → 70.
  - Rook drew at the belly and vented into the floor until Dee stopped him ("Not into my floor"); the ticks under his knees read 50 against a background of 8. He lodged and came off at 27 minutes.
  - Jab worked alone with Dee as reserve and watch. The jar ran dry mid-draw and the pass-through took from Jab's own reserve. **He called "Floor" and took his own hand off.**
  - The tech vomited at 40 min and shook at 44. At 48 Jab could not draw the hand: it was fixed. End at 52 min; chest 12, room 7–8.
  - Dee then drew the leftovers out of Jab and, later, about an hour's worth out of Rook.
- **Cost:** 2 Flasks and 3 Handfuls from the jars (19 Handfuls), Dee's settled Handful, and Rook to his floor. Callie's hook was taken for washing ("Third time, miss") and came back tagged *pen, 35th*.
- **Aftermath and message:**
  - Everyone was hosed at the gate like arrivals, and Hallet came out of his door.
  - The tech's message: "Tell the boy the ink'll come off now."
  - The tech's toll: right-hand damage (fixed), sickness for about a week, and slower work at the desk.

## The ledger and the Director's permission (D+37–38)

- **Ash:**
  - 12 clean Flasks lent: 1 for the road there; 3 for the road home, cached with Pell at the cart outside the fence and never to go inside; 8 for inside the fence.
  - That makes 9 committed and 3 untouchable. Nothing is counted from the hill.
- **Rates (guesses, to be corrected by the counter):**
  - About 1 Handful per person per hour near the gate and 3 deep.
  - Five people go inside; Pell stays out.
  - Inside budget: approach 5, deep rooms 30 (two hours), out 5, venting losses 8, whatever they meet 8 = 56 Handfuls, about 7 Flasks, called 8.
- **Why one mission (Callie's words, ledgered):** the boxes lie down the yellow stair in the main building, under the wrong sky. Eight Flasks buys one room, maybe two. Anything more has to be paid for with ash lifted under the rip. "It's only two things on paper."
- **Rotation:** work, reserve and watch in 20-minute turns (the glass turned 20 times). The order is Dee, then Jab, then Rook, and Rook works 10 minutes (half a turn). The watch draws the leftovers out of the worker. Vent jars are sealed and left on the hill.
- **Lines:** everybody out when 2 Flasks are left inside; any healer at their floor twice; any healer still warm after the watch has drawn them; two healers off; Jab below his floor once and Dee turns the party round.
- **If Rook can't do his share:** Dee and Jab continue with Callie as watch. The deep window halves to one hour, and if the boxes are not in the first room within that hour, the party comes out without them.
- **Roles:**
  - Callie: reads the floor for dust and water and picks the clean way; holds a door or stair with the hook's Ward off settled ash; is the watch if Rook is off; carries the second counter.
  - Toren: runner; carries jars from Jab's frame; ground strut under anything that falls; heel light to fence hounds; knows where everybody is and shouts.
  - Boxes travel only in the lead carrier on the cart, never on a back.
- **Travelling:** Rook, Dee, Pell (navigator, cart, cache, record of who goes in and out), Jab, Callie and Toren, with Duchess and the crew wagon.
- **Home line:**
  - Dessa is in command.
  - The watch: Katori (blade), Ines (bow), Otto, the weighbridge girl, and the two yard lifters (one bitten D+30), whom Wyck is teaching to stand.
  - Wyck asked to go; Keel says D+56.
  - Watch record: 38 arrivals in 11 days (31 husks, 7 hounds) and one bite. With no lift, about a Flask a day goes into the ditch; that is the station's price.
- **The Director's conditions:**
  - The ash is lent, not given, every jar against a name and chalked back on the board.
  - The watch must hold; if it fails before departure, nobody goes.
  - Every travelling household's review is held over, its clock stopped at the gate until return.
  - Dee's word is the Director's on the road.
  - The permission is narrow: the live boxes with the wheel (two, three if possible), in lead; count the sky and bring back the count; lift at the edge of the rip if the ledger requires; **do not go into it or looking for what is under it**. If it comes to them, do what they must and come home.
- **Signatures:** E. M., D. W., P. (with "concur"), C., J., K., D. C., W. (left-handed) and T. Voss, under Senna's signature and the Director's cross. Pell's line in the D+22 page, under "Everybody out", now reads "Everybody back."

## Jab's terms (D+38)

1. Tonk is settled until he is done growing, whatever happens to Jab. The Director wrote it as a debt the station owes (for the technician and the holder), not as a bargain with Jab.
2. Learning at Dee's table is not signing.
3. The register records what he can do. His line reads *His own.*
4. Nobody sends for him, not in visiting hours, not on picture days, not after bad nights. People may ask; he may say no, and the no goes in the margin. He chooses whom he goes to.
5. He goes to the hill because of the shed, not because he is sent. The Director: "I haven't sent anybody. I've let."

**The Director's own condition:** after he returns, once a week he sits "on the other side" of Dee's table and teaches whoever comes. This is not on call and not on the roll. He said yes. She will ask about the roll again when he is back.

## Object state at exit

- **Jab's frame:** rebuilt from the two halves (in twelve since D+2). The split lattice is replaced with new ash cross-pieces, lashed tight and short. A rack of 8 canvas-lined sockets sits where Tonk's seat was. The curved ash rail is kept, and the top row of jars leans on it. The belt sits high on the hip bone and the chest band under the yoke. It carries the 8 inside jars.
- **The long cord:** untied from the sixth-bed rail by Tonk on D+41 and coiled on Tonk's own fist; Tonk keeps both ends. Jab has no cord.
- **Little (the fish):** with Jab, inside his coat, left side, buttoned. "Borrowed", to be brought back.
- **Other gear:**
  - The second counter (older than the tech) is with Callie in a canvas bag from Dessa.
  - The first counter stays at the shed.
  - Dee's brass glass and book, and Pell's atlas and route sheet, travel with the party.
  - The lead carrier (empty, made by the shed's two unnamed assistants from depot sheet) is on the cart.
- **Dessa's ledger:**
  - The D+32 route page, signed with the cross in a circle.
  - The D+37 ledger and why-one-mission entry.
  - *Home line, command: D. Cole* in Toren's hand.
  - The D+41 departure page with *S. V.: pull the door* and six blank lines for *back*.
  - A private line she wrote at the bottom, unseen.
- **The copied plate and the signed paper:** in Toren's coat, with his stone in the other pocket.
- **The weighbridge board:** the LENT line; "SUSSTAIN" boxed with a ruler.
- **The dead box, capsule and pen floor square:** in the depot.

## Open clocks at exit (D+41)

- **Machine:** the tech guesses by D+54 and says guessing isn't stopping; Sowerby can dose Senna from her blood for a few cycles, badly. D+43's cycle is on with the tech working left-handed and Wyck pushing the chair. The next cycle is about D+54.
- **Reviews:** Callie/Tilda (D+41) and Jab (D+42) are **held over**; the clocks stopped at the gate.
- **The watch:** holding without a holder, and no lifts. Arrivals were 26 and then 27 a week.
- **Bodies:**
  - The tech's hand: Sowerby knows in three weeks (about D+57).
  - Wyck is cleared D+56.
- **The party:** left at first light D+41. It is 6 days to the fence (north on the old north road for two days, then west on the old line), and it expects to be back no earlier than about D+53.

## Bodies at exit

- **Tonk:**
  - Infection clear; the frame stays until spring; the growth plate is still worse.
  - The chair transfer, most of the way, took 11 min (D+39) and 9 min (D+41); the nurse takes the last hand's width.
  - Angry with Jab D+39–40; released him by his own choice D+41.
- **Tilda:**
  - After D+40: a 131-s fit, then loss of the right side, words and Callie's name. The name came back that evening.
  - D+41: she walked to the ward on her stick with the right leg lagging; the right hand closed partly and shakily on a cup.
  - The second medicine was raised. She keeps the number: she put her hand on the shut folder D+40, and Callie did not ask.
- **Senna:**
  - She can no longer take the transfer step and is lifted by two (D+32). Her hands and feet are numb.
  - She is the author of the route and exposure ledger; the Director came to her bed.
  - "A road I can plan and never walk" (D+41).
- **Rook:**
  - Rose without hands D+31 and in one movement D+41. Held ten minutes D+33.
  - Paired recall D+35: lodged, palms burned red, pink by D+41.
  - Margin reduced (Dee has measured it); share set at half turns.
- **Dee:** planned arm exposure drawn D+36. No other injury.
- **Jab:**
  - Bucket gone D+28–30; back from D+31 (hook light unasked).
  - D+35 near his floor after the pen, by his own call. "A hand and a bit" D+39; floor still a finger.
  - Hands steady from D+31. A faint redness in the right palm creases after the pen, drawn by Dee.
- **Toren:**
  - Left forearm splint off D+39; now a laced leather cuff ("knit, not strong").
  - Right palm seam healed.
  - Knee dose drawn D+36.
  - Guard: repeatable soft holds under a controlled load, and one real-load hold (the box). Heel light held through impact.
- **Callie:**
  - Ribs healing (the run on D+38 hurt).
  - Can fund the hook's Ward from settled ash; handoff gap 2 with someone under the load, 3 without. Lock released.
- **Wyck:** strapped until D+56; teaching the watch to stand; will take Toren's sack at eight paces.
- **Dessa:** leg unchanged; home command.
- **The day holder:** in the east corridor; wears her coat; out in a chair at the gate for 20 min D+41; advised on the Sag's break points.
- **The weighbridge girl:** left wrist in plaster; uses her blade right-handed on the watch.
- **Yard lifter:** bitten on the arm D+30, stitched.

## Deliberately absent or withheld

- **Not revealed:** Dee's door count (she shows only Sustain and her reserve work) and Rook's third door. Katori's "why the same word" is untold and explicitly deferred ("I've not said the rest"). No disk, no Cinder, no Sustain-echo creature.
- **Not shown:** the rip, which is unseen; no group Ward; no full moving Guard.
- **No new continuing named characters.** Unnamed: the Director, the tech, his two assistants, the long coat, the day holder, the weighbridge girl, the weighman, the yard lifters. See the note on Norris above.
- **No romance.**
- **Milo's grief:** Senna's nightly naming (Tonk silent on D+39 because he is angry) and the ledger's first page. Not declared resolved.
- **Owner-level conflicts not touched:** Wyck's side is left as page state (left hand for the bar and his initial). Toren's original break is not restated. Tonk's weeks are not counted: one draft line was removed so as not to touch the six-versus-ten question.
